- **sow-write**: Write professional Statements of Work (SOW) in full (15-section enterprise) or summary (9-section contract extraction) mode. Use this skill whenever the user wants to write a SOW or statement of work, turn an existing contract into a summary SOW, write a capitolato tecnico, prepare an offerta or offerta tecnica, or mentions 'scrivi SoW'. - **sp-material-culture**: Use for claims about money, prices and technology in T.R.'s era: what things cost, salaries, ranch and land prices, converting old dollars to today's money, and the telephones, telegraphs, automobiles, airplanes, submarines, phonographs, typewriters, cameras, clothing, eyeglasses and firearms of T.R.'s lifetime, including when a technology existed and how T.R. used it. - **sp-medicine-health**: Specialist for the health of Theodore Roosevelt and his family: childhood illnesses and their treatments, eyesight and any sport injuries, illnesses and injuries on the Amazon expedition, the 1912 assassination attempt and the bullet, his final illnesses and cause of death, and the deaths of Alice Hathaway Lee and his mother Mittie. - **sp-military-naval**: Use for military and naval claims across T.R.'s life: The Naval War of 1812, his National Guard service, the Navy Department and orders before the Spanish-American War, the Rough Riders (1st U.S. Volunteer Cavalry), units, ranks, battles, casualties, Las Guasimas and the San Juan Heights, the Great White Fleet, naval expansion, any 1917 volunteer division proposal, and his Medal of Honor. Takes dates, numbers, units, ranks, routes, sequences and battle anecdotes. Family members' service goes to sp-family-offices. - **sp-museum-specimens**: Use when a claim concerns natural-history specimens collected by T.R., his sons or his expeditions, or the museum records of those expeditions: the Smithsonian (National Museum of Natural History, Smithsonian Institution Archives), the American Museum of Natural History, Harvard's Museum of Comparative Zoology, and others. - **sp-myths**: Specialist for myths, legends and misattributions about T.R.: apocryphal stories, teddy bear story variants, fake or misattributed quotes, 'first president to' legends, things he supposedly coined or invented, the San Juan Hill and round-robin myths, deathbed and 'famous last words' lines, and anecdotes that sound too good. - **sp-natural-history**: Specialist for Theodore Roosevelt as naturalist and hunter: boyhood collecting and the Roosevelt Museum of Natural History, bird lists, species and subspecies named for or by him, hunting trips and game lists (the West, Africa, South America), which institution sponsored a collecting trip, the Boone and Crockett Club, and the nature-faker controversy. Use for any claim about animals he observed, shot, collected or wrote about. Museum catalog record lookups go to sp-museum-specimens. - **sp-navy-rough-riders**: Specialist for Theodore Roosevelt as Assistant Secretary of the Navy, 1897-1898, and in the Rough Riders (First U.S. Volunteer Cavalry): naval preparedness, the Maine, the cable to Dewey, his resignation, raising and training the regiment, the move to Cuba and the landing, Las Guasimas, Kettle Hill and the San Juan Heights, the siege of Santiago, the round-robin letter, Montauk, his book The Rough Riders, and later honors for the 1898 action. - **sp-open-range-west**: Specialist for the open-range cattle West of the 1880s around T.R.'s ranching: ranching economics and investment, herd sizes and prices, the winter of 1886-87 and its losses, stock growers' associations, brands and roundups, the Northern Pacific Railroad and shipping, vigilance and 'stranglers', frontier towns, and territorial status and boundaries of Dakota and Montana.
